FULL PAPER VERIFICATION TRACKER - Cambridge O Level Biology 5090, Papers 1 and 2
Generated 2026-11-11 17:39 UTC from the persistent verification state.

STATUS CODES (question level)
VERIFIED          = question text and its official mark-scheme section (Paper 2) / answer key (Paper 1) were read in full by the verifier, the syllabus subsection was confirmed, and (Paper 1) the key was checked against the stem and options. Marking points go to the notes subsection(s) shown.
IN-SCOPE-PENDING  = classified into a requested subsection but NOT yet verified question-by-question.
PENDING-REVIEW    = automatic classification only; not yet reviewed.
OUT-OF-SCOPE      = question read (stem + key / stem + start of mark scheme) and classified by the verifier as belonging to a syllabus section that was not requested (code shows which); its marking points are deliberately NOT in the notes.
Out-of-scope codes: o2 = Classification (syllabus section 2); o6 = Plant nutrition (section 6); o8 = Human nutrition (section 8); o9 = Human gas exchange (section 9); o10 = Respiration (section 10); o12 = Disease and immunity (section 12); o16 = Development of organisms / reproduction / nuclear division (section 16); o18 = Biotechnology and genetic modification (section 18); o19 = Organisms and environment / ecology / human impact (section 19); oX = Legacy or other topic not in the supplied syllabus list (e.g. support and movement, drugs, kidney dialysis, ADH, growth)
Figure status: "figure viewed" = the diagram / graph / table was rendered from the PDF and inspected; "FIGURE NOT YET VIEWED" = answer depends on a figure that has not been inspected; "text sufficient / figure not rendered" = the extracted text (labels, tables, options) was sufficient to check the answer.
Marking points = number of separately credited points (";" separators) in the matched mark-scheme section(s); Paper 1 = 1 per question.

  Q30 key B | 14.6 | VERIFIED | figure viewed | FLAG: Ambiguous key (B). The graph of liver glycogen falls in both period B (from the peak back to the normal level) and period C (from normal to below normal); in both, glycogen is being broken down, which is the action of adrenaline. Using the logic of the similar item w23_12:27 (glycogen rising = blood glucose above average), blood glucose would be moving up towards its set point in both B and C. Key B has been recorded as given; option C cannot be excluded from the information printed. Notes record only the general rule (falling liver glycogen = glycogen being converted to glucose).
